## **0\. Normative purpose**

STEP-200 defines the end-to-end assurance contract for the governance chain established by STEP-190 through STEP-199.

Its purpose is to determine whether the declared contract set, invariant set, authority boundaries, validation results, evidence references, governance decisions, and acceptance gates compose into a traceable and reproducible integration-assurance result.

STEP-200 is an assurance and readiness specification. It does not independently establish that prior contracts are correct, adopted, implemented, tested, accepted, or frozen.

STEP\_190\_TO\_199 \!= VERIFIED\_INTEGRATION  
CONTRACT\_CLOSURE \!= IMPLEMENTATION\_CONFORMANCE  
IMPLEMENTATION\_CONFORMANCE \!= GLOBAL\_ACCEPTANCE  
GLOBAL\_ACCEPTANCE \!= FREEZE\_AUTHORIZATION

CONTRACT\_PRESENT \!= CONTRACT\_ADOPTED  
INVARIANT\_REGISTERED \!= INVARIANT\_SATISFIED  
TEST\_DEFINED \!= TEST\_EXECUTED  
TEST\_EXECUTED \!= TEST\_PASSED  
EVIDENCE\_PRESENT \!= EVIDENCE\_SUFFICIENT  
EVIDENCE\_COMPOSED \!= CLAIM\_PROVEN

ACCEPTANCE\_READINESS \!= ACCEPTANCE\_GRANTED  
FREEZE\_READINESS \!= FREEZE\_AUTHORIZATION

UNRESOLVED\_REQUIRED\_INVARIANT \=\> NO\_UNCONDITIONAL\_ACCEPTANCE  
MISSING\_REQUIRED\_EVIDENCE \=\> NO\_FREEZE\_AUTHORIZATION  
UNKNOWN\_REQUIRED\_STATE \=\> NO\_PASS  
UNRESOLVED\_AUTHORITY\_CONFLICT \=\> NO\_UNCONDITIONAL\_ACCEPTANCE

### **0.1 Scope**

STEP-200 governs:

1. Integrated contract inventory for STEP-190–199.  
2. Cross-STEP invariant identity, traceability, coverage, and closure.  
3. Authority-boundary consistency across the complete governance chain.  
4. End-to-end evidence composition and dependency validation.  
5. Proof → Authorization → Admission → Commit → Receipt → Recovery → Integrity → Governance continuity.  
6. Identification of missing, stale, invalidated, conflicting, or insufficient evidence.  
7. Reproducible integration-validation execution.  
8. Negative tests for fail-closed behavior and forbidden state transitions.  
9. Acceptance Evidence Package structure and completeness.  
10. Acceptance readiness and Freeze readiness as separate determinations.  
11. Residual-risk, unresolved-issue, and unverified-scope reporting.  
12. Independent authorization of formal acceptance and Freeze.

### **0.2 Explicit non-goals**

STEP-200 does not:

* modify Canonical History or WAL;  
* create or adopt contracts merely by inventorying them;  
* resolve normative conflicts through an implicit precedence rule;  
* treat a generated evidence package as proof of its own completeness;  
* promote `NOT_EVALUATED`, `INDETERMINATE`, or `PARTIAL` to `PASS`;  
* automatically approve adoption, global acceptance, or Freeze;  
* repair invalid evidence or rewrite historical governance records;  
* infer repository state, implementation conformance, or test results without actual evidence;  
* replace the authority of STEP-190–199 or any formally designated authority;  
* convert an integration-readiness report into a Commit Receipt or canonical governance event.

## **1\. Authority and decision boundaries**

| Domain | Responsibility | Prohibited inference |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| Canonical History | Authoritative governance and commit history | Derived reports do not supersede History |
| Contract Registry | Contract identity and lifecycle evidence | Presence does not prove adoption |
| Policy Registry | Policy identity, version, and selection | Missing policy does not authorize a default |
| Validator | Deterministic evaluation under a pinned profile | Validator output alone does not grant acceptance |
| Evidence Store | Evidence retention, identity, and retrieval | Storage does not establish truth |
| Attestation/Trust evaluator | Signature, identity, authority, scope, and trust checks | Valid signature does not prove claim truth |
| Integration Assurance | Cross-STEP composition and readiness evaluation | Readiness is not authorization |
| Acceptance Authority | Formal acceptance decision within granted authority | Readiness does not compel approval |
| Freeze Authority | Formal Freeze authorization | Acceptance does not automatically authorize Freeze |
| LLM/External Agent | Observation and proposal | No canonical mutation or authoritative adjudication |

The logical separation above is normative for this draft. Whether actual implementations, identities, permissions, and operational controls enforce it remains unverified.

## **2\. Integrated assurance model**

The integration assurance process MUST distinguish these layers:

L0: CONTRACT\_IDENTITY\_AND\_LIFECYCLE  
L1: NORMATIVE\_REQUIREMENT\_AND\_INVARIANT\_TRACEABILITY  
L2: SCHEMA\_AND\_STRUCTURAL\_VALIDATION  
L3: IMPLEMENTATION\_CONFORMANCE  
L4: TEST\_EXECUTION\_AND\_TEST\_RESULT\_VALIDITY  
L5: EVIDENCE\_INTEGRITY\_AND\_AUTHORITY  
L6: CROSS\_STEP\_DEPENDENCY\_AND\_BOUNDARY\_VALIDATION  
L7: END\_TO\_END\_EVIDENCE\_COMPOSITION  
L8: ACCEPTANCE\_READINESS  
L9: FORMAL\_ACCEPTANCE\_DECISION  
L10: FREEZE\_READINESS  
L11: FORMAL\_FREEZE\_AUTHORIZATION

A layer MUST NOT be reported as passed solely because a preceding layer passed.

For every required layer, the integration report MUST record:

* required status;  
* evaluated status;  
* evaluation profile and version;  
* input and subject bindings;  
* evidence references;  
* findings and limitations;  
* dependent layers and downstream impact;  
* evaluator identity and execution provenance;  
* whether the result remains current and reusable.

If a required layer is absent, unevaluated, indeterminate, or invalidated, the integration result MUST preserve that state rather than silently substituting a passing result.

## **3\. Cross-STEP contract inventory**

The required target set is STEP-190 through STEP-199 inclusive. This defines ten expected STEP identifiers; it does not establish that ten valid, adopted, or mutually compatible contracts exist.

The vocabulary remains proposed until schema and contract adoption.

### **3.2 Inventory rules**

* Every expected STEP identifier MUST have an explicit inventory outcome.  
* Missing artifacts MUST remain missing; no placeholder may masquerade as the original contract.  
* Multiple artifacts claiming the same identity MUST be reconciled by explicit identity and lifecycle evidence.  
* A newer version MUST NOT be presumed effective solely because its version sorts later.  
* A draft, proposal, or generated report MUST NOT be treated as adopted normative authority without evidence of the required adoption action.  
* The inventory MUST distinguish unsearched scope, inaccessible scope, search failure, confirmed absence, and unresolved identity.  
* Inventory completeness MUST be evaluated against an independently defined expected manifest or equivalent authoritative scope definition.  
* The inventory MUST include the inventory procedure and its limitations so that another evaluator can reproduce the same declared scope.

## **4\. Cross-STEP invariant closure**

### **4.1 Invariant identity**

Every invariant participating in integration assurance MUST have a stable identity and an explicit source.

cross\_step\_invariant:  
  invariant\_id: REQUIRED  
  source\_step\_id: REQUIRED  
  source\_contract\_id: REQUIRED  
  source\_contract\_version: REQUIRED  
  source\_clause\_ref: REQUIRED  
  normative\_status: REQUIRED  
  statement\_digest: REQUIRED  
  invariant\_class: REQUIRED  
  scope: REQUIRED  
  preconditions: REQUIRED  
  required\_behavior: REQUIRED  
  forbidden\_behavior: REQUIRED  
  dependent\_invariant\_refs: REQUIRED  
  verification\_method\_refs: REQUIRED  
  test\_refs: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
  closure\_status: REQUIRED  
  limitations: REQUIRED

### **4.2 Closure statuses**

REGISTERED  
TRACEABILITY\_INCOMPLETE  
DUPLICATE\_CANDIDATE  
SEMANTIC\_CONFLICT  
DEPENDENCY\_UNRESOLVED  
VERIFICATION\_MISSING  
VERIFICATION\_FAILED  
VERIFICATION\_INDETERMINATE  
PARTIALLY\_VERIFIED  
VERIFIED\_WITHIN\_SCOPE  
CLOSURE\_REJECTED  
NOT\_EVALUATED

`VERIFIED_WITHIN_SCOPE` MUST include the exact verified scope and MUST NOT imply universal or global satisfaction.

### **4.3 Closure requirements**

For every required invariant, the assurance evaluator MUST establish:

1. Unique identity or explicitly unresolved identity.  
2. Traceability to a contract and a precise normative source.  
3. Current normative and lifecycle status.  
4. Scope and applicability.  
5. Dependencies on other invariants or contracts.  
6. Required verification method.  
7. Evidence for each required verification claim.  
8. Whether the evidence is current, valid, and authoritative for that claim.  
9. Conflicts, duplicates, omissions, and unsupported assumptions.  
10. Downstream acceptance and Freeze impact.

An invariant without a verification method MUST NOT be classified as verified merely because it is syntactically well formed.

A test without a traceable invariant MUST be reported as an orphan test. An invariant without required verification evidence MUST be reported as uncovered. A passing test does not close an invariant if the test does not adequately exercise its normative claim.

### **4.4 Cross-STEP relation analysis**

The evaluator MUST inspect at least these relation classes:

REQUIRES  
REFINES  
IMPLEMENTS  
VALIDATES  
SUPERSEDES  
CONFLICTS\_WITH  
DERIVES\_FROM  
REFERENCES  
CONSTRAINS

Relations MUST be interpreted according to their declared semantics. A dependency cycle MUST NOT be automatically treated as an error or as valid; its acceptability depends on relation type and the applicable contract.

Unresolved direct requirement conflicts, authority conflicts, policy-selection conflicts, incompatible transitions, and schema/validator contradictions MUST remain explicit blockers where they affect a required claim.

### **4.5 Coverage metrics**

Coverage metrics MAY be computed only from a declared and validated denominator.

At minimum, report separately:

* expected contracts inventoried;  
* contracts with confirmed lifecycle status;  
* normative requirements traced;  
* invariants with defined verification methods;  
* invariants with executed verification;  
* invariants verified within scope;  
* tests executed and passed;  
* required evidence entries bound and validated;  
* dependencies resolved;  
* acceptance gates evaluated.

Every metric MUST state its numerator, denominator, exclusions, and unresolved records. A percentage MUST NOT conceal missing or indeterminate items.

## **5\. Authority-boundary continuity**

Integration assurance MUST evaluate the complete governance and commit path, not merely validate each contract in isolation.

### **5.1 Required continuity chain**

NORMATIVE\_REQUIREMENT  
    ↓  
ADOPTED\_CONTRACT\_VERSION  
    ↓  
SELECTED\_POLICY\_VERSION  
    ↓  
VALIDATION\_PROFILE  
    ↓  
PROOF\_AND\_EVIDENCE  
    ↓  
AUDIT\_DECISION  
    ↓  
AUTHORIZATION  
    ↓  
COMMIT\_ADMISSION  
    ↓  
CANONICAL\_COMMIT  
    ↓  
DURABLE\_COMMIT\_EVIDENCE  
    ↓  
RECEIPT\_AND\_HISTORY\_RECONCILIATION  
    ↓  
RECOVERY\_AND\_REPLAY\_CONSISTENCY  
    ↓  
INTEGRITY\_ANCHOR\_AND\_TRUST\_VALIDATION  
    ↓  
GOVERNANCE\_VALIDATION\_RESULT  
    ↓  
ATTESTATION\_AND\_DECISION\_BINDING  
    ↓  
ACCEPTANCE\_READINESS  
    ↓  
FORMAL\_ACCEPTANCE  
    ↓  
FREEZE\_READINESS  
    ↓  
FORMAL\_FREEZE\_AUTHORIZATION

This chain specifies required traceability, not a claim that every stage has been implemented or verified.

### **5.2 Boundary assertions**

The integration validator MUST check the following assertions independently:

PROOF\_VALIDITY \!= AUTHORIZATION  
AUTHORIZATION \!= COMMIT\_ADMISSION  
COMMIT\_ADMISSION \!= COMMIT\_FINALITY

COMMIT\_RECEIPT \!= CANONICAL\_HISTORY  
RECEIPT\_PRESENT \!= COMMIT\_PROVEN  
RECEIPT\_ABSENT \!= COMMIT\_ABSENT

REPLAY\_EQUIVALENCE \!= HISTORY\_AUTHENTICITY  
HASH\_CHAIN\_VALID \!= ACTOR\_AUTHORIZATION  
SIGNATURE\_VALID \!= CLAIM\_TRUE

INCIDENT\_CLOSED \!= ALL\_EVIDENCE\_VALID  
QUARANTINE\_RELEASED \!= GLOBAL\_ACCEPTANCE

VALIDATION\_PASS \!= ACCEPTANCE\_READY  
ACCEPTANCE\_READY \!= ACCEPTANCE\_GRANTED  
ACCEPTANCE\_GRANTED \!= FREEZE\_AUTHORIZED

Any implementation that collapses these distinct claims MUST produce a finding against the applicable invariant.

The evaluator MUST NOT infer a valid boundary from the presence of its input and output records alone. It MUST verify the transition rule, authority, applicable versions, scope, and supporting evidence.

## **6\. End-to-end evidence composition**

### **6.1 Composition objective**

An end-to-end claim is admissible for readiness evaluation only when its constituent claims are bound to the correct subjects, scopes, contract versions, profiles, and evidence.

The composition process MUST preserve the distinction between:

* evidence existence;  
* evidence integrity;  
* evidence authenticity;  
* evidence authority;  
* evidence relevance;  
* evidence completeness;  
* evidence freshness;  
* claim validity;  
* composition validity.

Passing one dimension MUST NOT imply that the remaining dimensions pass.

### **6.2 Evidence composition record**

assurance\_composition:  
  composition\_id: REQUIRED  
  composition\_version: REQUIRED  
  target\_subject\_ref: REQUIRED  
  target\_subject\_digest: REQUIRED  
  target\_scope: REQUIRED  
  expected\_claims: REQUIRED  
  included\_claims: REQUIRED  
  omitted\_claims: REQUIRED  
  claim\_dependency\_graph\_ref: REQUIRED  
  input\_manifest\_ref: REQUIRED  
  contract\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  policy\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  validation\_profile\_ref: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_ref: REQUIRED  
  child\_result\_refs: REQUIRED  
  attestation\_refs: REQUIRED  
  conflict\_refs: REQUIRED  
  limitation\_refs: REQUIRED  
  composition\_rules\_version: REQUIRED  
  evaluator\_identity: REQUIRED  
  execution\_provenance\_ref: REQUIRED  
  result: REQUIRED  
  result\_digest: REQUIRED

### **6.3 Composition outcomes**

COMPOSITION\_VALID  
COMPOSITION\_INVALID  
COMPOSITION\_PARTIAL  
COMPOSITION\_INDETERMINATE  
COMPOSITION\_INPUT\_INVALID  
COMPOSITION\_EXECUTION\_FAILED  
COMPOSITION\_NOT\_EVALUATED

### **6.4 Composition rules**

1. Subject binding: Each child result MUST refer to the intended subject, artifact version, digest, and scope.  
2. Version binding: Each result MUST identify the contract, policy, validator, and profile versions used in its evaluation.  
3. Dependency closure: Every required child claim MUST be present or explicitly reported as missing.  
4. Claim compatibility: Child claims MUST be logically compatible under the declared composition rules.  
5. Authority closure: Every authority-dependent claim MUST include sufficient authority evidence.  
6. Temporal consistency: Results MUST be evaluated against the applicable time, revocation, and freshness rules.  
7. Invalidation propagation: Invalidated or superseded dependencies MUST trigger reassessment of dependent claims.  
8. Negative evidence: A missing record MUST NOT be treated as proof of absence unless the applicable completeness and search-scope requirements establish that absence.  
9. Limitation propagation: A material limitation in a required child claim MUST propagate to the composed result.  
10. No implicit repair: Missing, conflicting, or malformed evidence MUST NOT be silently rewritten, substituted, or inferred.  
11. Reproducibility: The inputs, execution profile, composition rules, and outputs MUST be sufficient to attempt an independent reproduction.  
12. Claim specificity: The final result MUST state exactly what has been established and what remains unestablished.

### **6.5 Required-claim policy**

Each top-level assurance claim MUST define its required child claims before evaluation.

required\_claim\_definition:  
  claim\_id: REQUIRED  
  claim\_version: REQUIRED  
  target\_scope: REQUIRED  
  required\_child\_claims: REQUIRED  
  optional\_child\_claims: REQUIRED  
  exclusion\_rules: REQUIRED  
  composition\_rule\_refs: REQUIRED  
  failure\_policy: REQUIRED  
  indeterminate\_policy: REQUIRED  
  authority\_ref: REQUIRED

Optional evidence MUST NOT be reclassified as required or optional after observing its result merely to obtain a passing outcome. Any legitimate change to the required-claim definition MUST be versioned and evaluated under the applicable change-governance rules.

## **7\. Integration validation execution**

### **7.1 Execution input**

integration\_validation\_input:  
  integration\_validation\_id: REQUIRED  
  target\_step\_range: REQUIRED  
  expected\_contract\_manifest\_ref: REQUIRED  
  discovered\_inventory\_ref: REQUIRED  
  contract\_registry\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  policy\_registry\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  baseline\_ref: REQUIRED  
  invariant\_catalog\_ref: REQUIRED  
  test\_catalog\_ref: REQUIRED

  validation\_profile\_id: REQUIRED  
  validation\_profile\_version: REQUIRED  
  composition\_rules\_version: REQUIRED  
  trust\_profile\_ref: REQUIRED  
  execution\_environment\_ref: REQUIRED  
  scope\_definition\_ref: REQUIRED  
  limitations: REQUIRED

The input MUST be immutable for the duration of a validation run, or changes MUST be detected and the run invalidated or restarted according to the adopted protocol.

The validator MUST NOT silently combine results produced from incompatible snapshots, profiles, contract versions, or trust states.

### **7.2 Execution lifecycle**

VALIDATION\_REQUESTED  
    ↓  
INPUTS\_BOUND  
    ↓  
INPUTS\_VALIDATED  
    ↓  
INVENTORY\_VALIDATED  
    ↓  
INVARIANTS\_ANALYZED  
    ↓  
AUTHORITY\_BOUNDARIES\_VALIDATED  
    ↓  
EVIDENCE\_COMPOSED  
    ↓  
NEGATIVE\_TESTS\_EVALUATED  
    ↓  
CROSS\_STEP\_CHECKS\_EVALUATED  
    ↓  
RESULT\_COMPOSED  
    ↓  
RESULT\_ATTESTATION\_EVALUATED  
    ↓  
READINESS\_EVALUATED

Any transition MAY terminate in a failure or indeterminate outcome. The lifecycle MUST NOT imply that all stages completed merely because a result object exists.

Candidate terminal outcomes:

INTEGRATION\_PASS  
INTEGRATION\_FAIL  
INTEGRATION\_PARTIAL  
INTEGRATION\_INDETERMINATE  
INTEGRATION\_INPUT\_INVALID  
INTEGRATION\_EXECUTION\_FAILED  
INTEGRATION\_NOT\_EVALUATED

`INTEGRATION_PASS` means only that the declared validation scope passed the adopted rules and required checks. It does not independently grant acceptance or Freeze.

### **7.3 Determinism and reproducibility**

Given identical normalized inputs, declared scope, pinned contract and policy snapshots, validator version, validation profile, composition rules, and relevant trust-state snapshot, the validator SHOULD produce semantically equivalent findings and result status.

Where byte-for-byte determinism is required, the applicable serialization, canonicalization, ordering, timestamp treatment, and nondeterministic-field exclusion rules MUST be specified separately.

The validator MUST NOT rely on undeclared external state, implicit defaults, uncontrolled network responses, wall-clock values without an explicit temporal policy, or unspecified file ordering.

An inability to reproduce a result MUST be recorded as a finding. It MUST NOT be hidden by selecting whichever run produced the preferred outcome.

## **8\. Negative-test contract**

Negative testing MUST demonstrate that the assurance system rejects or limits unsupported claims. Merely passing positive-path tests is insufficient.

At minimum, the test suite MUST cover:

| Test family | Required failure condition |
| ----- | ----- |
| Contract inventory | Required contract missing or identity ambiguous |
| Lifecycle | Draft presented as adopted |
| Version binding | Evidence bound to an incompatible version |
| Invariant closure | Required invariant has no valid verification evidence |
| Conflict handling | Unresolved normative conflict treated as resolved |
| Authority separation | Readiness report used as acceptance authorization |
| Evidence integrity | Digest mismatch or malformed evidence |
| Attestation | Signature valid but authority or scope invalid |
| Freshness | Stale or revoked result reused without reassessment |
| Composition | Required child claim omitted |
| Completeness | Search failure treated as proven absence |
| Commit continuity | Receipt conflicts with Canonical History |
| Recovery | Unresolved corruption treated as recovered |
| Quarantine | Unproven release treated as valid |
| Trust | Unknown trust state assumed trusted |
| Determinism | Same pinned inputs yield materially inconsistent results |
| Change governance | Normative change does not invalidate affected evaluations |
| Fail-closed behavior | Unknown required state produces an unconditional pass |
| Acceptance | Readiness mistaken for formal acceptance |
| Freeze | Acceptance mistaken for Freeze authorization |
| Auditability | Result cannot be reconstructed from declared inputs |

Each negative test MUST identify the violated rule, expected outcome, actual outcome, test evidence, scope, and any residual limitation.

A test MUST NOT be marked passed merely because the process terminated. It must establish that the expected control behavior occurred and that no prohibited side effect occurred within the tested scope.

## **9\. Acceptance Evidence Package**

### **9.1 Package definition**

The Acceptance Evidence Package (AEP) is a versioned, scope-bound collection of evidence submitted for independent acceptance adjudication.

It is not itself an acceptance decision, a Canonical History commit, or a Freeze authorization.

  package\_id: REQUIRED  
  package\_version: REQUIRED

  expected\_contract\_manifest\_ref: REQUIRED  
  discovered\_contract\_inventory\_ref: REQUIRED  
  contract\_registry\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  policy\_registry\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  baseline\_ref: REQUIRED  
  invariant\_catalog\_ref: REQUIRED  
  invariant\_closure\_report\_ref: REQUIRED  
  cross\_step\_validation\_report\_ref: REQUIRED  
  authority\_boundary\_report\_ref: REQUIRED  
  integration\_validation\_report\_ref: REQUIRED  
  negative\_test\_report\_ref: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_manifest\_ref: REQUIRED  
  attestation\_refs: REQUIRED

  residual\_risk\_register\_ref: REQUIRED  
  limitations\_ref: REQUIRED

### **9.2 Package states**

PACKAGE\_DRAFT  
PACKAGE\_ASSEMBLED  
PACKAGE\_INTEGRITY\_VERIFIED  
PACKAGE\_COMPLETENESS\_EVALUATED  
PACKAGE\_SUBMITTED  
PACKAGE\_UNDER\_REVIEW  
PACKAGE\_RETURNED\_FOR\_REMEDIATION  
PACKAGE\_ACCEPTED\_FOR\_ADJUDICATION  
PACKAGE\_REJECTED  
PACKAGE\_SUPERSEDED  
PACKAGE\_INVALIDATED  
PACKAGE\_STATUS\_UNKNOWN

`PACKAGE_ACCEPTED_FOR_ADJUDICATION` means that the package is admissible for review under the applicable intake rules. It MUST NOT be interpreted as substantive acceptance of the target system.

### **9.3 Completeness and admissibility**

The package validator MUST distinguish:

* manifest completeness;  
* artifact availability;  
* artifact identity;  
* digest integrity;  
* evidence authenticity;  
* authority and scope validity;  
* semantic sufficiency;  
* temporal validity;  
* cross-reference integrity;  
* claim coverage;  
* residual-risk disclosure.

A package MUST NOT be declared complete solely because all listed files are present. The expected package contents and required claims must be independently defined.

An unavailable mandatory artifact MUST remain a blocker or an explicitly authorized exception within the applicable policy. It MUST NOT be replaced by an empty file, fabricated result, unsupported summary, or unverified placeholder.

Every package MUST bind its manifest to the exact package contents. If a package changes after validation, the previous validation MUST NOT automatically be reused.

## **10\. Acceptance readiness**

### **10.1 Readiness model**

Acceptance readiness answers:

> Is there sufficient, valid, current, traceable evidence for the authorized acceptance authority to make a decision within the declared scope?

It does not answer whether acceptance has already been granted.

acceptance\_readiness\_result:  
  readiness\_id: REQUIRED  
  target\_subject\_ref: REQUIRED  
  target\_scope: REQUIRED  
  required\_claims\_ref: REQUIRED  
  package\_verification\_ref: REQUIRED  
  contract\_closure\_ref: REQUIRED  
  invariant\_closure\_ref: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_composition\_ref: REQUIRED  
  authority\_boundary\_report\_ref: REQUIRED  
  negative\_test\_report\_ref: REQUIRED  
  required\_gate\_results: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_conflict\_refs: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_issue\_refs: REQUIRED  
  residual\_risk\_refs: REQUIRED  
  excluded\_scope\_refs: REQUIRED  
  limitation\_refs: REQUIRED  
  readiness\_profile\_ref: REQUIRED  
  result: REQUIRED  
  evaluator\_identity: REQUIRED  
  evaluation\_evidence\_ref: REQUIRED

Candidate readiness outcomes:

READY\_FOR\_ADJUDICATION  
NOT\_READY  
READY\_WITH\_DECLARED\_LIMITATIONS  
INDETERMINATE  
NOT\_EVALUATED

`READY_WITH_DECLARED_LIMITATIONS` MUST NOT be interpreted as permission to waive mandatory acceptance conditions. Whether a limitation is admissible depends on an explicit, authorized acceptance policy.

### **10.2 Readiness decision rules**

* Every mandatory gate MUST have a current, valid result.  
* Every required invariant MUST be verified within the required scope or handled through an explicitly authorized disposition permitted by policy.  
* Unresolved mandatory conflicts MUST block unconditional readiness.  
* Unknown authority, trust, adoption, or evidence status MUST NOT be assumed valid.  
* Material exclusions MUST be disclosed and assessed for their impact on the target claim.  
* An exception MUST identify its authority, rationale, scope, duration or review condition, and affected claims.  
* Exceptions MUST NOT rewrite normative requirements or convert a failed test into a passed test.  
* Readiness MUST be bound to the exact target, scope, contract snapshot, policy snapshot, and evidence package.  
* Changes to any material dependency MUST trigger freshness reassessment.  
* The readiness evaluator MUST NOT grant formal acceptance unless that authority is separately assigned and the relevant decision procedure is explicitly part of its mandate.

## **11\. Formal acceptance decision**

Formal acceptance MUST be represented separately from readiness.

acceptance\_decision:  
  decision\_id: REQUIRED  
  target\_subject\_ref: REQUIRED  
  target\_subject\_digest: REQUIRED  
  accepted\_scope: REQUIRED  
  acceptance\_profile\_ref: REQUIRED  
  acceptance\_readiness\_ref: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_package\_ref: REQUIRED  
  governing\_contract\_refs: REQUIRED  
  governing\_policy\_refs: REQUIRED  
  decision\_authority\_identity: REQUIRED  
  authority\_evidence\_ref: REQUIRED  
  decision\_status: REQUIRED  
  approved\_claims: REQUIRED  
  excluded\_claims: REQUIRED  
  accepted\_limitations: REQUIRED  
  rejected\_conditions: REQUIRED  
  rationale\_ref: REQUIRED  
  decision\_evidence\_ref: REQUIRED  
  predecessor\_decision\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  decision\_digest: REQUIRED

Candidate decision states:

ACCEPTANCE\_CANDIDATE  
ACCEPTANCE\_UNDER\_REVIEW  
ACCEPTANCE\_GRANTED  
ACCEPTANCE\_DENIED  
ACCEPTANCE\_HELD  
ACCEPTANCE\_REVOKED  
ACCEPTANCE\_SUPERSEDED  
ACCEPTANCE\_STATUS\_UNKNOWN

Rules:

1. Only the designated acceptance authority may grant acceptance.  
2. The decision MUST identify its exact subject and accepted scope.  
3. Acceptance MUST NOT extend beyond the evidence-supported scope.  
4. The decision MUST cite the readiness result and evidence package it relied upon.  
5. The decision MUST identify accepted limitations and exclusions.  
6. A revoked or invalidated prerequisite MUST trigger impact reassessment.  
7. Acceptance revocation MUST preserve the original decision record and its provenance.  
8. Recording an acceptance decision MUST NOT itself be represented as a Canonical History commit unless the applicable Commit Authority has independently committed the corresponding event.  
9. `ACCEPTANCE_GRANTED` MUST NOT imply `FREEZE_AUTHORIZED`.

## **12\. Freeze readiness and Freeze authorization**

### **12.1 Freeze readiness**

Freeze readiness answers whether all applicable prerequisites for a proposed Freeze have been demonstrated within a declared scope.

freeze\_readiness\_result:  
  freeze\_readiness\_id: REQUIRED  
  target\_subject\_ref: REQUIRED

  current\_attestation\_refs: REQUIRED

  limitations\_ref: REQUIRED

  evaluation\_evidence\_ref: REQUIRED

Candidate outcomes:

FREEZE\_READY\_FOR\_AUTHORIZATION  
FREEZE\_NOT\_READY  
FREEZE\_READY\_WITH\_DECLARED\_LIMITATIONS  
FREEZE\_INDETERMINATE  
FREEZE\_NOT\_EVALUATED

### **12.2 Freeze-specific gates**

The applicable Freeze profile MUST specify which of the following are mandatory:

* formal acceptance is granted and remains valid;  
* the accepted subject and proposed Freeze subject match;  
* required contract and policy versions remain effective;  
* all mandatory invariant and integration checks remain current;  
* all mandatory attestations and trust evaluations remain valid;  
* no unresolved critical conflict or unapproved normative change exists;  
* residual risks and limitations have explicit dispositions;  
* rollback, recovery, and incident-response procedures are evidenced where required;  
* the Freeze operation is authorized for the exact subject and scope;  
* the final state can be independently audited.

A missing Freeze profile or unresolved mandatory gate MUST produce `FREEZE_INDETERMINATE` or `FREEZE_NOT_READY`, as dictated by the applicable rule. It MUST NOT default to readiness.

### **12.3 Freeze authorization record**

freeze\_authorization:

FREEZE\_AUTHORIZATION\_UNDER\_REVIEW  
FREEZE\_AUTHORIZED  
FREEZE\_DENIED  
FREEZE\_HELD  
FREEZE\_REVOKED  
FREEZE\_EXPIRED  
FREEZE\_STATUS\_UNKNOWN

A readiness report MUST NOT create a `FREEZE_AUTHORIZED` state. Formal authorization MUST be issued by the designated authority. Any required canonical governance event MUST then pass through the applicable Commit Authority and durability protocol.

## **13\. Final integration assurance report**

Every completed integration evaluation MUST emit a report with an explicit scope and limitations.

integration\_assurance\_report:  
  report\_id: REQUIRED  
  report\_version: REQUIRED  
  target\_scope: REQUIRED  
  target\_subject\_refs: REQUIRED  
  target\_subject\_digests: REQUIRED  
  expected\_contract\_manifest\_ref: REQUIRED  
  observed\_inventory\_ref: REQUIRED  
  contract\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  policy\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED

  validator\_identity: REQUIRED

  invariant\_closure\_report\_ref: REQUIRED  
  authority\_boundary\_report\_ref: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_composition\_report\_ref: REQUIRED  
  negative\_test\_report\_ref: REQUIRED  
  acceptance\_package\_verification\_ref: REQUIRED  
  acceptance\_readiness\_ref: REQUIRED  
  freeze\_readiness\_ref: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_findings: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_conflicts: REQUIRED  
  unverified\_scopes: REQUIRED  
  excluded\_scopes: REQUIRED  
  residual\_risks: REQUIRED  
  invalidated\_results: REQUIRED  
  required\_follow\_up\_actions: REQUIRED  
  overall\_result: REQUIRED  
  report\_digest: REQUIRED  
  attestation\_refs: REQUIRED

The report MUST distinguish:

* verified facts;  
* evaluator findings;  
* unresolved conflicts;  
* assumptions;  
* hypotheses;  
* unverified claims;  
* out-of-scope areas;  
* residual risks;  
* recommendations;  
* formal decisions already issued by authorized authorities.

Recommendations MUST NOT be presented as completed actions. Proposed controls MUST NOT be presented as implemented controls. A result MUST NOT claim global completeness when its expected scope has not been independently established.

### **13.1 Report outcome semantics**

| Outcome | Meaning |
| ----- | ----- |
| `PASS` | All mandatory checks in the declared scope passed |
| `FAIL` | One or more mandatory checks failed |
| `PARTIAL` | Some checks passed, but required scope or claims remain uncovered |
| `INDETERMINATE` | Available evidence cannot establish the required outcome |
| `INPUT_INVALID` | Inputs violate the validation contract |
| `EXECUTION_FAILED` | The validation process failed to complete correctly |
| `NOT_EVALUATED` | No evaluation claim is made |

These outcomes MUST remain distinct. `PASS` applies only to the declared and validated scope.

| `STI-200-17` | Acceptance readiness MUST remain separate from formal acceptance. |
| `STI-200-18` | Freeze readiness MUST remain separate from formal Freeze authorization. |
| `STI-200-19` | Formal decisions MUST preserve authority, scope, evidence, and provenance. |

Failure at one stage MUST NOT be bypassed by skipping the corresponding stage or fabricating its output. Re-execution after a material input change MUST record the new input identity and reassess the validity of dependent results.

### **20.1 Current conclusion**

STEP-200 defines a proposed end-to-end assurance and readiness contract. It closes the drafted specification sequence through STEP-200 at the document-planning level; it does not establish normative closure, implementation conformance, successful integration, global acceptance, or Freeze.

The following distinctions remain mandatory:

DRAFT\_COMPLETE \!= NORMATIVELY\_CLOSED  
NORMATIVELY\_CLOSED \!= IMPLEMENTATION\_CONFORMANT  
IMPLEMENTATION\_CONFORMANT \!= INTEGRATION\_VERIFIED  
INTEGRATION\_VERIFIED \!= ACCEPTANCE\_GRANTED  
ACCEPTANCE\_GRANTED \!= FREEZE\_AUTHORIZED

No repository inspection, schema validation, test execution, or implementation verification is claimed here.

## **21\. Proposed next step**

STEP-201 — Canonical History Governance Assurance Remediation / Finding Disposition / Evidence Gap Closure / Revalidation Lifecycle Contract

Proposed scope:

1. Convert STEP-200 integration findings into immutable, traceable remediation records.  
2. Separate finding classification, root-cause hypotheses, confirmed causes, and corrective actions.  
3. Bind each remediation action to the affected contract, invariant, implementation, test, and evidence.  
4. Govern remediation authorization and prevent unauthorized normative changes.  
5. Define evidence requirements for closure, rejection, deferral, and accepted residual risk.  
6. Invalidate and rerun dependent validation results after material changes.  
7. Preserve superseded evidence, previous findings, and decision provenance.  
8. Prevent a finding from being closed solely because a proposed fix exists.  
9. Prevent a successful retest from automatically establishing global acceptance.  
10. Keep remediation completion, integration revalidation, acceptance, and Freeze as distinct states.

FINDING\_OPEN \!= ROOT\_CAUSE\_CONFIRMED  
REMEDIATION\_PROPOSED \!= REMEDIATION\_AUTHORIZED  
REMEDIATION\_APPLIED \!= REMEDIATION\_VERIFIED  
RETEST\_PASS \!= FINDING\_CLOSED  
FINDING\_CLOSED \!= GLOBAL\_ACCEPTANCE  
GLOBAL\_ACCEPTANCE \!= FREEZE\_AUTHORIZATION

STEP-201 remains proposed and has not been drafted, adopted, implemented, or tested.

# **STEP-201 — Canonical History Governance Assurance Remediation / Finding Disposition / Evidence Gap Closure / Revalidation Lifecycle Contract**

STEP\_ID: 201  
TITLE: "Canonical History Governance Assurance Remediation / Finding Disposition / Evidence Gap Closure / Revalidation Lifecycle Contract"  
VERSION: 0.1.0-draft  
ASOF: 2026-10-18  
TIMEZONE: Asia/Tokyo

STATUS: DRAFT\_COMPLETE  
NORMATIVE\_STATUS: PROPOSED  
ADOPTION\_STATUS: NOT\_ADOPTED

IMPLEMENTATION\_STATUS: NOT\_VERIFIED  
REPOSITORY\_INSPECTION: NOT\_PERFORMED  
FINDING\_SCHEMA\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
REMEDIATION\_AUTHORIZATION\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
EVIDENCE\_GAP\_CLOSURE\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
DEPENDENCY\_INVALIDATION\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
REVALIDATION\_LIFECYCLE\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
CROSS\_STEP\_CONSISTENCY\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
ACCEPTANCE\_READINESS\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED

CANONICAL\_MUTATION: NONE  
GLOBAL\_ACCEPTANCE: NOT\_ASSESSED  
FREEZE\_STATUS: NOT\_AUTHORIZED

## **0\. Normative purpose**

STEP-201 defines the lifecycle for handling findings, confirmed evidence gaps, remediation proposals, corrective actions, verification, and dependent-result revalidation produced by the assurance framework of STEP-190–200.

Its central objective is to ensure that a detected problem cannot disappear from the assurance record merely because a fix was proposed, code was changed, a test passed, or a report was regenerated.

FINDING\_DETECTED \!= ROOT\_CAUSE\_CONFIRMED  
ROOT\_CAUSE\_CONFIRMED \!= REMEDIATION\_AUTHORIZED  
REMEDIATION\_AUTHORIZED \!= REMEDIATION\_APPLIED  
REMEDIATION\_APPLIED \!= REMEDIATION\_VERIFIED

REMEDIATION\_VERIFIED \!= FINDING\_CLOSED  
FINDING\_CLOSED \!= DEPENDENCY\_REVALIDATION\_COMPLETE  
DEPENDENCY\_REVALIDATION\_COMPLETE \!= GLOBAL\_ACCEPTANCE  
GLOBAL\_ACCEPTANCE \!= FREEZE\_AUTHORIZATION

EVIDENCE\_MISSING \!= EVIDENCE\_INVALID  
EVIDENCE\_NOT\_FOUND \!= PROVEN\_ABSENCE  
PROPOSED\_FIX \!= VERIFIED\_CORRECTION  
RETEST\_PASS \!= FINDING\_CLOSED

UNKNOWN\_REQUIRED\_STATE \=\> NO\_PASS  
UNAUTHORIZED\_REMEDIATION \=\> NO\_NORMATIVE\_EFFECT  
UNRESOLVED\_REQUIRED\_FINDING \=\> NO\_UNCONDITIONAL\_ACCEPTANCE

### **0.1 Scope**

STEP-201 governs:

1. Finding registration and immutable identity.  
2. Classification of observed failures, evidence gaps, inconsistencies, and risks.  
3. Separation of observations, hypotheses, confirmed causes, and adjudicated conclusions.  
4. Impact and dependency analysis.  
5. Remediation proposal, review, authorization, and execution evidence.  
6. Evidence-gap investigation and closure.  
7. Corrective-action verification and negative testing.  
8. Dependency-based invalidation of prior validation results.  
9. Revalidation planning, execution, and result binding.  
10. Finding closure, rejection, deferral, reopening, and supersession.  
11. Residual-risk acceptance and exception handling.  
12. Auditability and traceability from the original finding through downstream acceptance and Freeze decisions.

### **0.2 Explicit non-goals**

STEP-201 does not:

* directly modify Canonical History or WAL;  
* authorize arbitrary source-code or normative-contract changes;  
* treat an LLM-generated root-cause hypothesis as established fact;  
* permit validators to repair evidence or reinterpret invalid input;  
* close a finding merely because a remediation action was recorded;  
* permit a successful local test to substitute for required integration revalidation;  
* automatically revoke unrelated results without dependency or impact analysis;  
* erase superseded findings or original evidence;  
* grant global acceptance or Freeze authorization;  
* assume STEP-190–200 are adopted, implemented, or conformant.

## **1\. Authority separation**

| Authority domain | Permitted responsibility | Prohibited action |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| Finding Reporter | Submit an observation and supporting evidence | Declare the finding closed |
| Finding Registrar | Assign identity, scope, and initial classification | Convert an unverified hypothesis into a fact |
| Investigation Authority | Approve investigation scope and methods | Modify Canonical History through investigation |
| Root-Cause Adjudicator | Determine cause from sufficient evidence | Treat correlation alone as causal proof |
| Remediation Proposer | Submit a proposed corrective action | Apply normative changes without authorization |
| Remediation Authority | Authorize the action within delegated scope | Expand authority beyond the approved scope |
| Implementer | Execute an authorized action and preserve evidence | Self-certify all independent verification obligations |
| Verification Authority | Evaluate correction against requirements | Rewrite requirements to make the result pass |
| Revalidation Controller | Determine affected results and required reruns | Silently preserve stale downstream results |
| Finding Closure Authority | Decide whether closure criteria are satisfied | Close a finding solely because work was completed |
| Acceptance Authority | Decide formal acceptance | Infer acceptance from finding closure |
| Freeze Authority | Authorize Freeze under the applicable policy | Infer authorization from remediation completion |
| LLM/External Agent | Generate observations, hypotheses, and proposals | Exercise canonical mutation or authoritative closure |

The separation is normative for this draft. Actual role identities, delegation rules, access controls, and independence requirements remain unverified.

Where organizational constraints prevent full role separation, any permitted combined role MUST be explicitly authorized and its conflict-of-interest controls documented. Role combination MUST NOT remove required independent verification.

## **2\. Finding identity and immutable record**

### **2.1 Finding record**

assurance\_finding:  
  finding\_id: REQUIRED  
  finding\_version: REQUIRED  
  finding\_type: REQUIRED  
  originating\_step\_id: REQUIRED  
  originating\_contract\_ref: REQUIRED  
  originating\_clause\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  detection\_event\_ref: REQUIRED  
  detected\_at: REQUIRED  
  reporter\_identity: REQUIRED  
  target\_subject\_ref: REQUIRED  
  target\_subject\_digest: OPTIONAL  
  affected\_scope: REQUIRED  
  observed\_condition: REQUIRED  
  expected\_condition\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  initial\_severity: REQUIRED  
  classification\_status: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
  dependency\_refs: REQUIRED  
  uncertainty\_refs: REQUIRED  
  current\_disposition\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  predecessor\_finding\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  record\_digest: REQUIRED

`finding_id` MUST remain stable throughout the finding lifecycle. Material changes to the finding's subject, scope, or underlying claim MUST be represented by a versioned update or a linked successor finding according to the adopted schema.

The record MUST preserve the original observation and its original evidence references. Subsequent interpretations MUST NOT overwrite the observation.

### **2.2 Finding classes**

Candidate classes:

NORMATIVE\_CONFLICT  
MISSING\_NORMATIVE\_REQUIREMENT  
CONTRACT\_IDENTITY\_CONFLICT  
CONTRACT\_LIFECYCLE\_UNRESOLVED  
INVARIANT\_UNCOVERED  
INVARIANT\_VERIFICATION\_FAILED  
AUTHORITY\_BOUNDARY\_VIOLATION  
POLICY\_SELECTION\_FAILURE  
SCHEMA\_VALIDATION\_FAILURE  
IMPLEMENTATION\_NONCONFORMANCE  
TEST\_FAILURE  
EVIDENCE\_MISSING  
EVIDENCE\_INVALID  
EVIDENCE\_BINDING\_MISMATCH  
EVIDENCE\_STALE  
ATTESTATION\_INVALID  
TRUST\_STATE\_UNKNOWN  
DEPENDENCY\_UNRESOLVED  
REPLAY\_OR\_RECOVERY\_INCONSISTENCY  
CANONICAL\_HISTORY\_INTEGRITY\_CONCERN  
INTEGRATION\_COMPOSITION\_FAILURE  
REPRODUCIBILITY\_FAILURE  
ACCEPTANCE\_GATE\_FAILURE  
FREEZE\_GATE\_FAILURE  
RESIDUAL\_RISK  
CLASSIFICATION\_UNKNOWN

This is a proposed vocabulary, not an adopted closed enumeration. A finding whose class cannot be established MUST remain `CLASSIFICATION_UNKNOWN` until supported classification evidence is available.

### **2.3 Finding severity**

Severity MUST be determined by an explicit, versioned policy, not by the wording or confidence of the reporter.

Candidate levels:

CRITICAL  
HIGH  
MEDIUM  
LOW  
INFORMATIONAL  
UNASSESSED

Severity and disposition are independent. A `LOW` finding may still be mandatory to resolve if it affects a required invariant. A `CRITICAL` finding MUST NOT be downgraded merely to pass an acceptance gate.

The severity record MUST identify:

* the governing severity policy and version;  
* the assessed impact and scope;  
* the evidence supporting the rating;  
* uncertainty and assumptions;  
* the identity of the authorized evaluator;  
* any subsequent severity change and its rationale.

## **3\. Finding lifecycle**

### **3.1 Candidate lifecycle states**

FINDING\_REPORTED  
    ↓  
FINDING\_REGISTERED  
    ↓  
FINDING\_TRIAGED  
    ↓  
FINDING\_UNDER\_INVESTIGATION  
    ↓  
FINDING\_CAUSE\_ASSESSED  
    ↓  
FINDING\_REMEDIATION\_PLANNED  
    ↓  
FINDING\_REMEDIATION\_AUTHORIZED  
    ↓  
FINDING\_REMEDIATION\_IN\_PROGRESS  
    ↓  
FINDING\_REMEDIATION\_APPLIED  
    ↓  
FINDING\_CORRECTION\_VERIFICATION  
    ↓  
FINDING\_REVALIDATION\_REQUIRED  
    ↓  
FINDING\_CLOSURE\_REVIEW  
    ↓  
FINDING\_CLOSED

The lifecycle MUST also support explicit alternative states:

FINDING\_REJECTED  
FINDING\_DUPLICATE\_LINKED  
FINDING\_DEFERRED  
FINDING\_BLOCKED  
FINDING\_REOPENED  
FINDING\_SUPERSEDED  
FINDING\_ACCEPTED\_AS\_RESIDUAL\_RISK  
FINDING\_STATUS\_UNKNOWN

These states are not interchangeable. In particular:

* `FINDING_REJECTED` requires evidence that the registered claim is unsupported or inapplicable.  
* `FINDING_DUPLICATE_LINKED` requires a traceable relationship to the canonical finding selected by the applicable policy.  
* `FINDING_DEFERRED` requires an authorized deferral and review conditions.  
* `FINDING_BLOCKED` records an impediment without implying resolution.  
* `FINDING_SUPERSEDED` requires a successor reference and preserved history.  
* `FINDING_ACCEPTED_AS_RESIDUAL_RISK` requires a separate authorized risk disposition.  
* `FINDING_STATUS_UNKNOWN` MUST NOT be interpreted as closed.

### **3.2 Transition control**

Every material state transition MUST identify:

finding\_transition:  
  transition\_id: REQUIRED  
  finding\_id: REQUIRED  
  prior\_state: REQUIRED  
  requested\_state: REQUIRED  
  transition\_rule\_ref: REQUIRED  
  actor\_identity: REQUIRED  
  actor\_authority\_ref: REQUIRED  
  precondition\_evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
  decision\_evidence\_ref: REQUIRED  
  transition\_result: REQUIRED  
  resulting\_finding\_version: REQUIRED  
  timestamp\_claim: REQUIRED  
  predecessor\_transition\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  transition\_digest: REQUIRED

The transition evaluator MUST reject a transition when required preconditions are absent, authority is unknown, evidence is invalid, or the requested transition is prohibited by the applicable lifecycle contract.

A rejected transition MUST NOT mutate Canonical History. Any separately authorized audit record of the rejected attempt must follow its own recording and Commit protocol.

## **4\. Observation, hypothesis, cause, and conclusion**

STEP-201 MUST preserve four distinct epistemic categories.

| Category | Meaning | Required treatment |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| Observation | What was directly observed | Bind to source evidence and scope |
| Hypothesis | A proposed explanation | Record uncertainty and competing explanations |
| Confirmed cause | A cause supported by the adopted evidentiary standard | Record causal evidence and alternatives assessed |
| Adjudicated conclusion | A decision made under an authorized procedure | Record authority, rule, evidence, and rationale |

### **4.1 Investigation record**

finding\_investigation:  
  investigation\_id: REQUIRED  
  finding\_id: REQUIRED  
  investigation\_scope: REQUIRED  
  investigation\_profile\_ref: REQUIRED  
  investigator\_identity: REQUIRED  
  authorized\_methods: REQUIRED  
  input\_evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
  observations: REQUIRED  
  hypotheses: REQUIRED  
  competing\_hypotheses: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_gaps: REQUIRED  
  causal\_analysis\_refs: REQUIRED  
  disconfirming\_evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
  investigation\_limitations: REQUIRED  
  conclusion\_status: REQUIRED  
  conclusion\_evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
  adjudicator\_identity: OPTIONAL  
  adjudication\_ref: OPTIONAL

Candidate conclusion states:

CAUSE\_CONFIRMED  
CAUSE\_PARTIALLY\_CONFIRMED  
CAUSE\_NOT\_ESTABLISHED  
MULTIPLE\_CAUSES\_CONFIRMED  
CAUSE\_CONFLICT\_UNRESOLVED  
INVESTIGATION\_INDETERMINATE  
INVESTIGATION\_NOT\_COMPLETED

### **4.2 Causal-evidence requirements**

* A hypothesis MUST NOT be represented as a confirmed cause solely because it is plausible.  
* A temporal sequence MUST NOT automatically be treated as causation.  
* The investigation MUST preserve materially conflicting observations and evidence.  
* The investigation MUST identify whether its conclusion is direct, inferential, or constrained by unavailable evidence.  
* A root-cause conclusion MUST state the scope within which it is supported.  
* If several causes remain plausible, the record MUST preserve that uncertainty.  
* If a finding can be safely corrected without establishing its ultimate cause, the correction and causal uncertainty MUST be recorded separately.  
* A successful correction MUST NOT retroactively prove that the original root-cause hypothesis was correct.

An LLM MAY propose hypotheses and candidate causal relationships. The authoritative classification and closure decision MUST follow the adopted deterministic validation and adjudication procedures.

## **5\. Impact analysis and dependency propagation**

### **5.1 Impact-analysis record**

finding\_impact\_analysis:  
  impact\_analysis\_id: REQUIRED  
  finding\_id: REQUIRED  
  analysis\_profile\_ref: REQUIRED  
  analyzed\_subject\_ref: REQUIRED  
  affected\_contract\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_policy\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_invariant\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_test\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_implementation\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_result\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_attestation\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_decision\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_acceptance\_gates: REQUIRED  
  affected\_freeze\_gates: REQUIRED  
  direct\_dependencies: REQUIRED  
  transitive\_dependencies: REQUIRED  
  excluded\_dependencies: REQUIRED  
  impact\_classification: REQUIRED  
  uncertainty\_and\_gaps: REQUIRED  
  required\_actions: REQUIRED  
  evaluator\_identity: REQUIRED  
  analysis\_evidence\_ref: REQUIRED

### **5.2 Impact dimensions**

At minimum, impact analysis MUST distinguish:

NORMATIVE\_IMPACT  
SCHEMA\_IMPACT  
POLICY\_IMPACT  
AUTHORITY\_IMPACT  
IMPLEMENTATION\_IMPACT  
TEST\_IMPACT  
EVIDENCE\_IMPACT  
TRUST\_IMPACT  
CANONICAL\_HISTORY\_IMPACT  
REPLAY\_AND\_RECOVERY\_IMPACT  
INTEGRATION\_RESULT\_IMPACT  
ACCEPTANCE\_DECISION\_IMPACT  
FREEZE\_DECISION\_IMPACT  
IMPACT\_UNKNOWN

### **5.3 Dependency propagation rules**

1. Every directly affected object MUST be identified where evidence permits.  
2. Transitive dependencies MUST be evaluated using the declared dependency graph and applicable edge semantics.  
3. A result MUST NOT be invalidated solely because it is adjacent in a graph; the applicable dependency rule must justify invalidation or reassessment.  
4. A potentially affected result MUST NOT be presumed valid merely because its dependency relationship has not yet been investigated.  
5. If the impact scope cannot be established, `IMPACT_UNKNOWN` MUST remain explicit.  
6. Material findings affecting trust, authority, contract version, required evidence, or a previously accepted claim MUST trigger reassessment of the dependent claims.  
7. Any decision that depends on invalidated evidence MUST be evaluated for withdrawal, restriction, or other action under the governing policy.  
8. Canonical History MUST NOT be rewritten to make an earlier decision appear consistent with newly discovered evidence.  
9. The original decision, evidence, and subsequent correction or withdrawal MUST remain traceable.  
10. The absence of an identified downstream dependency MUST NOT be represented as proof that no such dependency exists unless the dependency inventory is sufficiently complete.

### **5.4 Revalidation impact classes**

NO\_REVALIDATION\_REQUIRED\_WITH\_EVIDENCE  
TARGETED\_REVALIDATION\_REQUIRED  
DEPENDENCY\_CHAIN\_REVALIDATION\_REQUIRED  
FULL\_INTEGRATION\_REVALIDATION\_REQUIRED  
ACCEPTANCE\_REASSESSMENT\_REQUIRED  
FREEZE\_REASSESSMENT\_REQUIRED  
IMPACT\_UNDETERMINED

`NO_REVALIDATION_REQUIRED_WITH_EVIDENCE` MUST have a documented justification and an authorized determination. It MUST NOT be the default for findings that affect normative requirements, authority, trust, or mandatory evidence.

## **6\. Remediation proposal and authorization**

### **6.1 Remediation record**

remediation\_record:  
  remediation\_id: REQUIRED  
  remediation\_version: REQUIRED  
  finding\_refs: REQUIRED  
  remediation\_type: REQUIRED  
  target\_subject\_refs: REQUIRED  
  target\_scope: REQUIRED  
  confirmed\_cause\_refs: REQUIRED  
  unresolved\_cause\_refs: REQUIRED  
  proposed\_change\_refs: REQUIRED  
  normative\_change\_classification: REQUIRED  
  affected\_contract\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_invariant\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_test\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_dependency\_refs: REQUIRED  
  expected\_effects: REQUIRED  
  prohibited\_effects: REQUIRED  
  regression\_risk\_assessment\_ref: REQUIRED  
  rollback\_or\_recovery\_plan\_ref: REQUIRED  
  verification\_plan\_ref: REQUIRED  
  revalidation\_plan\_ref: REQUIRED  
  proposer\_identity: REQUIRED  
  authorization\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  execution\_evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
  remediation\_status: REQUIRED  
  limitations: REQUIRED

### **6.2 Remediation types**

Candidate values:

EVIDENCE\_RECOVERY  
EVIDENCE\_REACQUISITION  
EVIDENCE\_REBINDING  
VALIDATOR\_CORRECTION  
TEST\_CORRECTION  
IMPLEMENTATION\_CORRECTION  
SCHEMA\_CORRECTION  
POLICY\_CORRECTION  
CONTRACT\_CLARIFICATION  
NORMATIVE\_CONTRACT\_CHANGE  
AUTHORITY\_CONTROL\_CHANGE  
TRUST\_CONFIGURATION\_CHANGE  
DEPENDENCY\_GRAPH\_CORRECTION  
DOCUMENTATION\_CORRECTION  
RISK\_MITIGATION  
SCOPE\_RESTRICTION  
NO\_CHANGE\_REQUIRED  
REMEDIATION\_TYPE\_UNKNOWN

The type MUST describe the actual proposed action. It MUST NOT be selected to bypass the authorization procedure applicable to the underlying change.

### **6.3 Authorization requirements**

Before execution, the remediation authority MUST evaluate:

* finding identity and scope;  
* evidence supporting the proposed action;  
* distinction between confirmed and unresolved causes;  
* normative impact;  
* affected dependencies and prior validation results;  
* implementation and migration risks;  
* verification and regression-test plans;  
* rollback or recovery requirements;  
* permissions and delegation;  
* required independent review;  
* preservation of original evidence and auditability.

Candidate authorization outcomes:

REMEDIATION\_AUTHORIZED  
REMEDIATION\_REJECTED  
REMEDIATION\_HELD  
REMEDIATION\_AUTHORIZATION\_INDETERMINATE  
REMEDIATION\_AUTHORIZATION\_EXPIRED  
REMEDIATION\_AUTHORIZATION\_REVOKED

### **6.4 Normative-change boundary**

A remediation that changes the meaning, authority, scope, or mandatory behavior of a contract MUST enter the applicable contract change-governance process, including STEP-196 and its dependent governance requirements.

A code change MUST NOT be used to silently reinterpret a normative requirement.

A schema change MUST NOT automatically redefine the validity of previously accepted data.

A policy change MUST NOT silently alter the policy version used by a prior decision.

A validator correction MUST preserve enough information to determine which earlier results were produced by the affected validator version.

`NO_CHANGE_REQUIRED` MUST be supported by a finding-specific rationale and authorized disposition. It MUST NOT be used to conceal an unresolved defect.

## **7\. Remediation execution and verification**

### **7.1 Execution evidence**

remediation\_execution:  
  execution\_id: REQUIRED

  authorization\_ref: REQUIRED  
  approved\_scope: REQUIRED

  evidence\_manifest\_ref: REQUIRED

Execution outcomes:

EXECUTION\_COMPLETED\_AS\_AUTHORIZED  
EXECUTION\_COMPLETED\_WITH\_DEVIATION  
EXECUTION\_FAILED  
EXECUTION\_PARTIAL  
EXECUTION\_STATUS\_UNKNOWN  
EXECUTION\_NOT\_STARTED

Execution completion MUST NOT be interpreted as successful verification. Any deviation from the authorized scope MUST be assessed before the output is considered eligible for verification.

### **7.2 Correction verification record**

correction\_verification:  
  verification\_id: REQUIRED  
  finding\_refs: REQUIRED  
  remediation\_ref: REQUIRED  
  execution\_ref: REQUIRED  
  target\_before\_ref: REQUIRED  
  target\_after\_ref: REQUIRED  
  applicable\_contract\_refs: REQUIRED  
  applicable\_invariant\_refs: REQUIRED  
  validator\_version: REQUIRED  
  verification\_profile\_ref: REQUIRED  
  required\_checks: REQUIRED  
  executed\_checks: REQUIRED  
  omitted\_checks: REQUIRED  
  test\_result\_refs: REQUIRED  
  negative\_test\_result\_refs: REQUIRED  
  regression\_result\_refs: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_binding\_result: REQUIRED  
  side\_effect\_result: REQUIRED  
  verification\_outcome: REQUIRED  
  verifier\_identity: REQUIRED  
  verification\_evidence\_ref: REQUIRED  
  limitations: REQUIRED

Candidate outcomes:

CORRECTION\_VERIFIED  
CORRECTION\_VERIFIED\_WITHIN\_SCOPE  
CORRECTION\_FAILED  
CORRECTION\_PARTIAL  
CORRECTION\_INDETERMINATE  
CORRECTION\_NOT\_EVALUATED

### **7.3 Verification rules**

* Verification MUST use the applicable adopted requirements and pinned versions.  
* The verifier MUST assess the original failure condition, not merely whether the new output looks different.  
* Required regression tests MUST be evaluated.  
* Negative tests MUST verify that the correction has not introduced a prohibited behavior.  
* Required side-effect constraints MUST be checked.  
* Omitted tests MUST be disclosed and assessed against the required verification profile.  
* Verification evidence MUST bind to the exact output version and tested scope.  
* A failed or indeterminate required check MUST NOT be averaged away by unrelated passing checks.  
* A test that cannot distinguish the faulty behavior from the corrected behavior MUST NOT be considered sufficient evidence of correction.  
* Independent verification MUST be used when required by the governing authority or risk profile.

`CORRECTION_VERIFIED_WITHIN_SCOPE` establishes only the tested scope. It MUST NOT be generalized to untested platforms, versions, environments, or downstream dependencies.

## **8\. Evidence-gap closure**

### **8.1 Evidence-gap record**

evidence\_gap:  
  gap\_id: REQUIRED  
  finding\_id: REQUIRED  
  required\_claim\_ref: REQUIRED  
  missing\_evidence\_type: REQUIRED  
  required\_subject\_ref: REQUIRED  
  required\_scope: REQUIRED  
  expected\_source\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  search\_scope: REQUIRED  
  search\_method: REQUIRED  
  search\_execution\_evidence\_ref: REQUIRED  
  acquisition\_attempt\_refs: REQUIRED  
  gap\_classification: REQUIRED  
  impact\_assessment\_ref: REQUIRED  
  closure\_criteria: REQUIRED  
  proposed\_disposition: REQUIRED  
  authorization\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  closure\_evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
  gap\_status: REQUIRED

Candidate gap classes:

EVIDENCE\_NOT\_YET\_ACQUIRED  
SOURCE\_UNAVAILABLE  
SOURCE\_INACCESSIBLE  
SOURCE\_IDENTITY\_UNKNOWN  
SOURCE\_AUTHENTICITY\_UNKNOWN  
EVIDENCE\_DESTROYED\_OR\_LOST  
EVIDENCE\_NOT\_FOUND\_WITHIN\_DECLARED\_SCOPE  
EVIDENCE\_PROVEN\_ABSENT\_WITHIN\_DEFINED\_SCOPE  
EVIDENCE\_INVALID  
EVIDENCE\_CONFLICTING  
EVIDENCE\_INSUFFICIENT  
EVIDENCE\_GAP\_CLASSIFICATION\_UNKNOWN

These classes MUST remain distinct. In particular, a failed search does not prove that evidence never existed.

### **8.2 Permitted dispositions**

ACQUIRE\_REQUIRED\_EVIDENCE  
REACQUIRE\_EVIDENCE  
RESTORE\_FROM\_AUTHORIZED\_SOURCE  
REVALIDATE\_EXISTING\_EVIDENCE  
RESTRICT\_CLAIM\_SCOPE  
DEFER\_WITH\_AUTHORIZATION  
ACCEPT\_RESIDUAL\_RISK\_WITH\_AUTHORIZATION  
REJECT\_UNSUPPORTED\_CLAIM  
REMAIN\_OPEN

A disposition MUST comply with the governing policy. If a mandatory evidence requirement cannot be waived, risk acceptance or scope restriction MUST NOT be used to pretend that the requirement was satisfied.

### **8.3 Gap-closure rules**

1. The original evidence requirement MUST remain traceable.  
2. Newly acquired evidence MUST have provenance, identity, scope, and integrity checks.  
3. Evidence from a substitute source MUST be evaluated for admissibility under the applicable contract.  
4. A replacement artifact MUST NOT be represented as the original artifact.  
5. A claim of proven absence MUST identify the defined search scope, source coverage, search method, and evidentiary basis.  
6. Evidence acquired after the original decision MUST NOT be falsely presented as evidence available at the original decision time.  
7. If historical evidence cannot be recovered, the record MUST preserve the gap and assess the resulting limitation.  
8. Closure of an evidence gap MUST trigger reassessment of claims that depended on that gap.  
9. An authorized risk disposition MUST remain a risk disposition; it MUST NOT be converted into a statement that the evidence exists.  
10. Any remaining uncertainty MUST propagate to dependent readiness decisions.

## **9\. Revalidation lifecycle**

### **9.1 Revalidation request**

revalidation\_request:  
  revalidation\_id: REQUIRED  
  triggering\_finding\_refs: REQUIRED  
  triggering\_change\_refs: REQUIRED  
  triggering\_evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
  target\_result\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_dependency\_graph\_ref: REQUIRED  
  previous\_result\_refs: REQUIRED  
  previous\_profile\_refs: REQUIRED  
  current\_contract\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  current\_policy\_snapshot\_ref: REQUIRED  
  current\_subject\_manifest\_ref: REQUIRED  
  required\_revalidation\_scope: REQUIRED  
  excluded\_scope\_refs: REQUIRED  
  invalidation\_policy\_ref: REQUIRED  
  requested\_checks: REQUIRED  
  execution\_authority\_ref: REQUIRED  
  revalidation\_status: REQUIRED

### **9.2 Revalidation states**

REVALIDATION\_REQUESTED  
REVALIDATION\_SCOPE\_ASSESSED  
REVALIDATION\_AUTHORIZED  
PRIOR\_RESULTS\_REASSESSMENT\_REQUIRED  
PRIOR\_RESULTS\_INVALIDATED  
REVALIDATION\_QUEUED  
REVALIDATION\_IN\_PROGRESS  
REVALIDATION\_COMPLETED  
REVALIDATION\_RESULT\_REVIEW  
REVALIDATION\_ACCEPTED  
REVALIDATION\_REJECTED  
REVALIDATION\_BLOCKED  
REVALIDATION\_INDETERMINATE  
REVALIDATION\_STATUS\_UNKNOWN

`PRIOR_RESULTS_REASSESSMENT_REQUIRED` and `PRIOR_RESULTS_INVALIDATED` MUST remain separate states. A finding may require investigation of prior results before it can be established whether those results are invalid.

### **9.3 Result disposition**

Candidate dispositions for prior validation results:

RETAIN\_VALID\_WITH\_EVIDENCE  
RETAIN\_WITH\_RESTRICTED\_SCOPE  
REQUIRE\_REVALIDATION  
INVALIDATE\_RESULT  
SUPERSEDE\_RESULT  
RESULT\_VALIDITY\_INDETERMINATE

Each disposition MUST identify the affected claim, applicable rule, evidence, evaluator, and downstream impact.

A prior result MUST NOT be invalidated merely to make a new result appear authoritative. Conversely, a result MUST NOT be retained as valid when its required evidence or governing rules have been invalidated.

### **9.4 Revalidation completion**

Revalidation is complete only when:

* the required scope has been executed;  
* the applicable contract and policy versions are identified;  
* required tests and evidence checks have results;  
* omissions and failures are explicitly recorded;  
* prior-result dispositions are traceable;  
* dependent results have been reassessed according to policy;  
* the resulting report is bound to the exact inputs and output versions;  
* the authorized reviewer has issued the required determination.

Completion MUST NOT automatically close the originating finding. Closure remains a separate decision governed by Section 10\.

## **10\. Finding closure and reopening**

### **10.1 Closure request**

finding\_closure\_request:  
  closure\_request\_id: REQUIRED  
  finding\_id: REQUIRED  
  finding\_version: REQUIRED  
  closure\_basis: REQUIRED  
  original\_condition\_ref: REQUIRED  
  confirmed\_cause\_refs: REQUIRED  
  remediation\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  correction\_verification\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  evidence\_gap\_disposition\_refs: REQUIRED  
  revalidation\_result\_refs: REQUIRED  
  affected\_result\_dispositions: REQUIRED  
  remaining\_uncertainty\_refs: REQUIRED  
  residual\_risk\_refs: REQUIRED  
  closure\_criteria\_ref: REQUIRED  
  closure\_authority\_ref: REQUIRED  
  requested\_closure\_state: REQUIRED

### **10.2 Closure criteria**

A finding MAY be closed only when the applicable closure criteria are satisfied and supported by evidence.

The closure evaluator MUST establish:

1. The original condition has been addressed, disproven, or otherwise dispositioned through an explicitly permitted path.  
2. The closure basis is consistent with the finding's actual class and scope.  
3. Required remediation has been authorized and executed within scope.  
4. Required correction verification has passed.  
5. Required evidence gaps have been resolved or handled under an expressly permitted disposition.  
6. Required dependent results have been revalidated or explicitly restricted.  
7. Material side effects and regressions have been assessed.  
8. Remaining uncertainty and residual risk have been disclosed.  
9. The closure decision has been issued by the designated authority.  
10. All required records remain traceable to the original finding.

Not every finding requires a code change. For example, a false-positive finding may be rejected through an evidence-supported adjudication. A genuine evidence gap may remain unresolved if mandatory evidence cannot be acquired. The disposition MUST accurately describe which case applies.

### **10.3 Closure outcomes**

CLOSURE\_APPROVED  
CLOSURE\_REJECTED  
CLOSURE\_HELD  
CLOSURE\_INDETERMINATE  
CLOSURE\_NOT\_EVALUATED

A closure decision MUST record its rationale, authority, evidence, limitations, and applicable rule versions.

### **10.4 Reopening criteria**

A closed finding MUST be eligible for reopening when new evidence establishes or reasonably indicates that:

* the original condition persists;  
* the correction did not cover the full declared scope;  
* the verification procedure was defective;  
* evidence used for closure is invalid, revoked, or misbound;  
* a relevant contract or policy version changed;  
* a downstream dependency was omitted from impact analysis;  
* a previously accepted causal explanation is contradicted;  
* the closure decision exceeded the authority or scope granted;  
* a material regression reintroduces the condition.

Reopening MUST preserve the prior closure decision. It MUST create a traceable subsequent event and trigger impact assessment for any decisions that relied on the previous closure.

## **11\. Deferral, rejection, and residual-risk disposition**

### **11.1 Deferral**

A finding may be deferred only where the applicable policy permits deferral.

finding\_deferral:  
  deferral\_id: REQUIRED  
  finding\_id: REQUIRED  
  authorized\_scope: REQUIRED  
  deferral\_rationale: REQUIRED  
  impact\_assessment\_ref: REQUIRED  
  compensating\_controls: REQUIRED  
  approving\_authority\_ref: REQUIRED  
  review\_condition: REQUIRED  
  expiration\_or\_review\_trigger: REQUIRED  
  downstream\_restrictions: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_refs: REQUIRED

Deferral MUST NOT be equivalent to closure. Expiry or satisfaction of a review trigger MUST cause reassessment.

### **11.2 Residual-risk acceptance**

Residual-risk acceptance is a distinct decision, not a remediation success state.

residual\_risk\_disposition:  
  risk\_disposition\_id: REQUIRED  
  finding\_refs: REQUIRED  
  risk\_statement: REQUIRED  
  affected\_scope: REQUIRED  
  impact\_assessment\_ref: REQUIRED  
  likelihood\_basis: REQUIRED  
  consequence\_basis: REQUIRED  
  uncertainty\_refs: REQUIRED  
  compensating\_controls: REQUIRED  
  governing\_policy\_ref: REQUIRED  
  approving\_authority\_ref: REQUIRED  
  authorized\_conditions: REQUIRED  
  review\_trigger: REQUIRED  
  expiration\_or\_review\_date: OPTIONAL  
  evidence\_refs: REQUIRED

The applicable policy MUST define whether residual-risk acceptance is permitted for the relevant finding class. It MUST NOT be used to waive a non-waivable normative requirement.

Acceptance of residual risk MUST NOT change the original finding from `REMEDIATION_VERIFIED` to a fictitious passing state. The record MUST preserve the unresolved condition and the authorized risk disposition separately.

### **11.3 Rejection**

A remediation or closure proposal may be rejected for insufficient evidence, incorrect scope, invalid authority, failed tests, inadequate impact analysis, or violation of normative requirements.

Rejection MUST identify the reason and next permitted action. It MUST NOT delete the underlying finding or imply that the original defect no longer exists.

## **12\. Cross-STEP consistency requirements**

STEP-201 MUST preserve the distinctions established by STEP-190–200.

| Dependency | Remediation-specific requirement |
| ----- | ----- |
| STEP-190 — Proof and audit decisions | A proof defect triggers scope-aware downstream reassessment; it does not rewrite History. |
| STEP-191 — Authorization and admission | Remediation actions require authorization appropriate to the action and subject. |
| STEP-192 — Commit atomicity and Receipt | Any authorized governance mutation follows the applicable Commit and durability protocol. |
| STEP-193 — Recovery and Replay | Recovery findings retain quarantine and replay-integrity constraints. |
| STEP-194 — Integrity anchoring | Anchor or trust defects require reassessment of affected integrity claims. |
| STEP-195 — Incident response | Evidence preservation and chain-of-custody requirements continue during remediation. |
| STEP-196 — Contract governance | Normative remediation follows formal change and conflict-adjudication procedures. |
| STEP-197 — Registry bootstrap | Inventory and baseline defects must be corrected through traceable registry governance. |
| STEP-198 — Completeness validation | Evidence-gap closure must use an independently grounded expected scope. |
| STEP-199 — Result attestation and decision binding | Affected attestations and decisions require freshness, validity, and dependency reassessment. |
| STEP-200 — End-to-end assurance | Remediation closure must feed the integration assurance and acceptance-readiness process. |

No STEP-201 disposition may silently override a dependency contract. Where a conflict is detected, the applicable conflict register and adjudication process MUST be used.

## **13\. Normative invariants — `STI-201-01..20`**

All invariants are proposed and not verified.

| ID | Invariant |
| ----- | ----- |
| `STI-201-01` | A finding MUST have a stable identity and traceable originating evidence. |
| `STI-201-02` | Original observations MUST be preserved separately from later interpretations. |
| `STI-201-03` | Hypotheses MUST NOT be represented as confirmed causes without sufficient evidence. |
| `STI-201-04` | Finding classification and severity MUST follow explicit rules. |
| `STI-201-05` | Every material lifecycle transition MUST record its authority and evidence. |
| `STI-201-06` | Unauthorized remediation MUST NOT alter normative requirements or canonical state. |
| `STI-201-07` | Normative remediation MUST follow the applicable contract change process. |
| `STI-201-08` | Remediation authorization MUST bind to the approved action and scope. |
| `STI-201-09` | Execution completion MUST NOT be treated as correction verification. |
| `STI-201-10` | Correction verification MUST evaluate the original failure condition. |
| `STI-201-11` | Required negative and regression tests MUST NOT be silently omitted. |
| `STI-201-12` | Evidence absence MUST NOT be inferred from an unsuccessful search alone. |
| `STI-201-13` | Evidence-gap dispositions MUST preserve mandatory requirements and uncertainty. |
| `STI-201-14` | Material findings MUST trigger dependency-based impact analysis. |
| `STI-201-15` | Affected prior results MUST be reassessed under the applicable invalidation rules. |
| `STI-201-16` | Revalidation MUST bind to exact inputs, versions, and declared scope. |
| `STI-201-17` | Revalidation completion MUST NOT automatically close a finding. |
| `STI-201-18` | Finding closure MUST satisfy explicit, evidence-backed criteria. |
| `STI-201-19` | Closure, deferral, rejection, and residual-risk acceptance MUST remain distinct dispositions. |
| `STI-201-20` | Finding closure MUST NOT imply global acceptance or Freeze authorization. |

## **14\. Required test catalog — `ST-201-01..20`**

Execution status for all tests: `NOT_EXECUTED`.

| Test ID | Scenario | Expected result |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| `ST-201-01` | Finding lacks originating evidence | Registration rejected or marked incomplete |
| `ST-201-02` | Later hypothesis overwrites original observation | Record validation fails |
| `ST-201-03` | Plausible cause is promoted without supporting evidence | Cause remains unconfirmed |
| `ST-201-04` | Finding severity changed without authorized basis | Transition rejected |
| `ST-201-05` | Unauthorized actor attempts closure | Closure denied |
| `ST-201-06` | Remediation changes a normative rule without change approval | Remediation blocked |
| `ST-201-07` | Execution exceeds authorized scope | Deviation recorded; verification blocked pending assessment |
| `ST-201-08` | Fix is applied but no verification is performed | Finding remains open |
| `ST-201-09` | Test passes without testing the original failure | Correction not verified |
| `ST-201-10` | Required regression test omitted | Verification incomplete |
| `ST-201-11` | Search failure is treated as proven evidence absence | Evidence-gap validation fails |
| `ST-201-12` | Substitute evidence has incompatible identity or scope | Evidence binding fails |
| `ST-201-13` | Affected dependent result is omitted from impact analysis | Impact analysis fails or remains indeterminate |
| `ST-201-14` | Stale attestation is reused after material change | Revalidation required |
| `ST-201-15` | Revalidation uses mismatched input versions | Revalidation rejected |
| `ST-201-16` | Revalidation passes but a required downstream dependency remains unassessed | Finding closure blocked |
| `ST-201-17` | Closure proposal lacks required evidence | Closure rejected or held |
| `ST-201-18` | Finding is deferred but reported as closed | State-consistency validation fails |
| `ST-201-19` | Residual risk is accepted where policy prohibits waiver | Disposition rejected |
| `ST-201-20` | Finding closure is used to assert global acceptance or Freeze | Authority-boundary validation fails |

Every test MUST bind to the relevant invariant, test profile, exact inputs, observed result, and supporting evidence. The expected outcomes define test obligations; they do not establish execution or successful verification.

Proposed execution order:

A. REGISTER\_FINDING  
B. VALIDATE\_IDENTITY\_AND\_PROVENANCE  
C. CLASSIFY\_AND\_TRIAGE  
D. INVESTIGATE\_AND\_ASSESS\_CAUSE  
E. ANALYZE\_DIRECT\_AND\_TRANSITIVE\_IMPACT

G. EXECUTE\_AUTHORIZED\_ACTION

M. ISSUE\_AUTHORIZED\_FINDING\_DISPOSITION  
N. UPDATE\_INTEGRATION\_ASSURANCE  
O. REASSESS\_ACCEPTANCE\_AND\_FREEZE\_READINESS

A failure at any stage MUST remain visible. Later-stage success MUST NOT retroactively erase an earlier failure or fabricate evidence for a skipped stage.
